# M2a "Connect" Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Turn the M1 shell into an app that saves, tests and opens connections to PostgreSQL 14–18, MySQL 8.0/8.4, MariaDB LTS and SQLite. That means:
- native drivers behind one `Driver` trait;
- profiles in `connections.toml`, with secrets in the OS keyring;
- URL paste in both directions;
- an ordered connect pipeline with honest error classes and quick fixes;
- the approved M2a connection-flow UI, in EN and TR, through Fluent.

**Architecture:** M2a adds eight core crates under `crates/core/` and one test-support crate:
- `hacivat-proto`: shared ids and enums.
- `hacivat-net`: byte streams, direct transport, the loopback forwarder, and non-interactive TLS.
- `hacivat-secrets`: keyring store, retention, scrubbing, pgpass/pg_service, and auth providers.
- `hacivat-driver`: the `Driver` trait, engine catalog and registry, URLs, sanitizing, and error classification.
- `hacivat-driver-pg`, `hacivat-driver-mysql`, `hacivat-driver-sqlite`.
- `hacivat-session`: the core Tokio runtime, profiles and their store, the connect pipeline, test connection, `ConnectionManager`, and the budget seam.
- `hacivat-testkit`: Docker targets, used by tests only.

The app gets a `connections/` module that joins these crates to new `hacivat-ui` primitives through shell hook slots. Core crates never see UI types. UI crates never see core types. The app is the only place where both meet (§5.1, enforced by `xtask check-deps`).

**Tech Stack:**
- Everything from M1: Rust 1.98.1, edition 2024, GPUI fork `4858c09`, Fluent and ICU4X, `jiff` 0.2.38.
- Runtime: `tokio` 1 (rt-multi-thread, net, time, sync, macros, io-util), `tokio-util` 0.7 (`CancellationToken`), `futures` 0.3.
- Drivers: `tokio-postgres` 0.7, `mysql_async` (latest; 0.37 at the time of writing, `default-features = false`, feature `default-rustls-ring`), `rusqlite` (latest; 0.42 at the time of writing, with features `bundled` and `column_metadata`).
- TLS: `rustls` 0.23 with the `ring` provider only (`default-features = false`; aws-lc-rs and aws-lc-sys are banned in `deny.toml`, P5), `tokio-rustls` 0.26 (`default-features = false`, `ring`), `rustls-platform-verifier`, `rustls-pki-types` (PEM), `rustls-native-certs` (MySQL platform roots only, P10).
- Secrets: `keyring` 3 with `apple-native`, `windows-native`, `async-secret-service`, `crypto-rust` and `tokio` (without `apple-native` the macOS store silently falls back to keyring's non-persistent mock; Task 4 tests for it); `secrecy` 0.10; `zeroize`.
- Data: `uuid` 1 (v4, serde), `url` 2, `percent-encoding` 2, `regex` 1.
- Dev-only: `rcgen` (test certificates), `tempfile`, `yaml-rust2` (testkit's compose check).
- Exact versions are whatever is current and compatible when Task 1 adds them; the record is `Cargo.lock`. Task 1 Step 3's manifest table is exhaustive (P2). Any other crate needs a ruling.

**Execution (PO, 2026-11-12):** subagent-driven, in parallel waves of 3–5 worktree agents. Branch `m2a` starts from `m1-shell` without merging `main` (SDD ledger, last entry).

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-12-hacivat-v1-design.md`. Read:
- the decision log, especially L1, L3, L13 and **L15**;
- the §4 rows "Engines and drivers", "Connections and profiles", "Network security", "Secrets storage" and "Safety and read-only";
- §5.1, §5.3, §5.4, §5.6, §5.7 and §5.8;
- §6 M2, §7, and §10 C10, C16 and C19.

Also read:
- `docs/decisions/open-product-decisions.md`: D-004, D-005, D-016, D-035, D-036, D-037, D-085.
- `docs/research/algorithms/01-connectivity-and-sessions.md`: HV-DRV-01..05, HV-CONN-01, -03, -07, HV-SEC-02 (the TLS modes only), HV-SEC-03, -04, -05, and §5.
- `design/m2-connect/NOTES.md` and `design/m2-connect/index.html`. **Both are binding for the UI.** That includes the PO answers at the end of NOTES.md.
- `DESIGN.md` and `.impeccable/design.json`.
- `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-12-m1-foundations-app-shell.md`, Global Constraints, which are inherited.
- `<main checkout>/.superpowers/sdd/2026-11-12-m1-foundations-app-shell/progress.md`, for lessons and deferred items, and `<main checkout>/.superpowers/sdd/2026-11-12-m2a-connect/progress.md`, the M2a ledger, whose rulings bind. Both by absolute main-checkout path: `.superpowers/` is git-ignored, so worktrees do not contain it (P24).
- `docs/research/datagrip/02-connectivity-and-drivers.md`, only as data about what users expect.

## Global Constraints

- **Inherited from M1, unchanged.** These are in the M1 plan's Global Constraints:
  - the GPUI pin and the "never read Zed GPL crates" rule;
  - toolchain 1.98.1; `license = "Apache-2.0"`, `publish = false`, `[lints] workspace = true`;
  - the no-`unsafe` rule;
  - the casing ban (INV-3, R6): only ASCII casing methods are allowed;
  - the text rules (INV-1, I18N-23);
  - the Fluent ID grammar and the prefix ⇔ file rule;
  - semantic tokens only;
  - the named rules (11 px floor, One Filled Badge, radii {0,3,4,6,9999}, popover-only shadow, 1 px blue focus ring, no ambient motion);
  - the shell metrics;
  - the stubs rule (F31). Stubs are also warning-free under `clippy -D warnings`: unused parameters are `_`-prefixed, and a private placeholder field is either used or carries `#[allow(dead_code, reason = "Task N")]` (P3);
  - the test-bodies rule (F9). Every test in this plan has literal asserts; an implementer never commits a comment-bodied or tautological test (P21, P33).
- **Layering.** All new `crates/core/*` crates are the Core layer. `hacivat-testkit` is Core and is only ever a dev-dependency. Only `hacivat-app` and `hacivat` may depend on both Core and UI. `hacivat-mcp` does not exist yet; when it does, it must link no driver or secret code (§5.1 rule 4).
- **Dependencies (F23, P2).**
  - Task 1 writes every new manifest with every dependency M2a needs, following the exact per-crate table in Task 1 Step 3: internal edges, Tokio features, dev-dependencies, `default-features = false` on `rustls`, `tokio-rustls` and `mysql_async`, and `hacivat-driver/fake` as a dev-dependency of `hacivat-app`.
  - A later task never edits a manifest it does not own. Task 1 also writes `deny.toml` (P1, P5); no later task edits it. The controller adds one-line fixes.
  - **Never run a plain `cargo update`** (M1 Task 15 lesson: it downgrades `windows-sys`). Add a dependency by editing the manifest and running `cargo check`, which adds only the new lock entries. If a precise bump is needed, use `cargo update -p <crate> --precise <v>`.
  - "Only additions" in `Cargo.lock` means no existing entry is downgraded or removed. A required upward bump of an existing entry is allowed, and is listed in the commit body (P46).
- **Licences.**
  - The cargo-deny allow-list is unchanged (§3.1.5).
  - `webpki-roots` (CDLA-Permissive-2.0) is certain to enter: `mysql_async`'s `rustls-tls` feature always pulls it. Task 1 adds a `[[licenses.exceptions]]` entry scoped to that one crate (ledger ruling, PO-away). Task 14 records the licence tree in ADR 0002 (P1).
  - rustls uses the `ring` provider only. `deny.toml` bans `aws-lc-rs` and `aws-lc-sys`: with two rustls providers compiled in, `ClientConfig::builder()` (which `mysql_async` calls internally) panics, and aws-lc-sys is under the OpenSSL licence. `hacivat_net::tls::install_crypto_provider()` installs ring once; `CoreRuntime::new` calls it (P5).
  - `rustls-native-certs` (already in `Cargo.lock`, licence accepted) is used only by `hacivat-driver-mysql`, for the OS roots (P10).
- **Secrets.** These are hard rules:
  - A password, passphrase or key never appears in `connections.toml`, `settings.toml`, `workspace.json`, logs, panic messages, `Debug` output, Display or Copy URLs, the clipboard (except an explicit "copy with password", which M2a does not offer), test output or test snapshots.
  - Secrets travel as `secrecy::SecretString` and are zeroized on drop. A type that holds one derives `Debug` only (redacted), never `PartialEq`; tests use `matches!` or accessors (P6).
  - Keyring calls run only on the Tokio blocking pool, never on the UI thread.
  - The keyring is never touched at startup. A secret is read only when a connect needs it. `SecretStore::keyring_available()` returns a flag computed once on the core runtime the first time a connection dialog opens; it never probes D-Bus or the keychain on the UI thread (P36).
  - `keyring` is built with `apple-native`, `windows-native`, `async-secret-service`, `crypto-rust` and `tokio`. Without them keyring silently uses its non-persistent mock store; Task 4 has a test that fails if the backend is the mock.
  - Dev builds use the `hacivat-dev/` keyring service prefix (R7) and never a plaintext store.
  - Exception, recorded in ADR 0002 (P49): inside the driver crates the password is copied into library-owned buffers that Hacivat cannot zeroize (`mysql_async` keeps it as a `String` in `Opts`, which derives `Debug`; `tokio-postgres` keeps it as bytes in `Config`, whose `Debug` redacts it). Driver code builds these values at the last moment, drops them right after the handshake, and never `Debug`-formats `Opts`, `Conn` or any value that holds them.
- **Threading (§5.4).**
  - All driver, network, file and keyring I/O runs on the `CoreRuntime` (Tokio, threads named `hv-core-*`). Profile and secret file writes reuse the ordered-writer pattern, spawned on the `CoreRuntime` handle, not on the GPUI background executor (P48).
  - GPUI foreground tasks await only `tokio::sync` channels (`oneshot`, `watch`, `mpsc`).
  - Tokio I/O types are never polled outside that runtime.
  - The UI thread never awaits I/O.
  - One bounded exception, at quit (P15): GPUI waits for `on_app_quit` futures for only 200 ms (`SHUTDOWN_TIMEOUT`, `gpui/src/app.rs:80`). The quit handler therefore calls `ConnectionManager::shutdown_blocking(shutdown_budget)` synchronously (a `std::sync::mpsc` reply with `recv_timeout`, 2 s), then `CoreRuntime::shutdown(&self, budget)`, which uses `shutdown_timeout`. The runtime is never dropped plainly, because a blocked keychain call would hang the exit.
  - GPUI tests that wait on core work use the shared `settle(vcx, d)` helper: `allow_parking()`, a real `std::thread::sleep(d)`, then `run_until_parked()`. UI clocks (test strip, capsule) read `cx.background_executor().now()` captured when a run starts, never `Progress.started`, so `advance_clock` drives them in tests (P22).
- **Machine text.**
  - URL, DSN and option-key parsing, version parsing and error classification are locale-invariant. They use only `eq_ignore_ascii_case` and `to_ascii_lowercase`.
  - Classification uses codes and never localized server text.
  - Core crates produce `(ErrClass, ErrArgs)` and never English sentences.
  - Server text is shown verbatim in its own field (I18N-14).
  - Production suggestion (ledger ruling, PO-away; P7): `production_hint` splits the host, then the name, into tokens on `[-._/:0-9]` and compares whole tokens with `eq_ignore_ascii_case` against `prod`, `prd`, `live` and `production` (`production` reports `"prod"`). It never matches substrings, so `delivery-db` gives `None`. It returns the token and a `HintSource { Host, Name }`.
- **Fluent IDs for M2a.** The mock IDs (NOTES.md "Copy strings") are the starting point. Map them as follows. Placeholders `{x}` become `{ $x }`, and every message with variables has a `# Variables:` block. The Turkish entries are NOTES.md's Turkish column, each with a `# Review: PO` comment. Small caps are pre-cased in the FTL.

  | Mock ID group | Repo ID | File |
  |---|---|---|
  | `app-palette`, `explorer`, `filter-conn`, `home-search`, `sb-noconn`, `cap-none`, `env-prod-cap`, `exp-empty-title` | reuse the M1 IDs `hv-titlebar-palette`, `hv-panel-explorer`, `hv-explorer-filter`, `hv-status-no-connection`, `hv-capsule-no-connection`, `hv-capsule-env-production`, `hv-explorer-empty` (no duplicates, P42) | `hacivat-ui.ftl` |
  | `env-*`, `acc-*`, `path-*`, `enc-*`, `kv-*`, `secret-*`, `sheet-*`, `unenc-*`, `toast-*`, `menu-*`, `del-*`, `prod-*`, `fix-*`, `err-*` chrome (`err-title`, `err-attempt`, `err-new-pw`, `err-tech`, `err-stage`, `err-class`, `err-code`, `err-server`, `err-elapsed`, `err-copy`) | `hv-conn-<mock id>`, e.g. `prod-title` → `hv-conn-prod-title` | `hacivat-ui.ftl` |
  | `dlg-*`, `auth-*`, `ret-*`, `pgpass-*`, `tls-*` (mode, CA and summary only), `adv-*`, `test-*`, `diag-*`, `step-*`, `url-*` | `hv-conn-<mock id>`; the section titles `sec-server`, `sec-auth`, `sec-tls`, `sec-adv` become `hv-conn-section-{server,auth,tls,advanced}` | `hacivat-ui.ftl` |
  | `home-*`, `exp-*`, `sb-env`, `cap-connecting`, `cap-failed`, `cap-retry` | `hv-home-*`, `hv-explorer-*`, `hv-status-env`, `hv-capsule-{connecting,failed,retry}` | `hacivat-ui.ftl` |
  | per-class headings and bodies (`err-auth-h`/`-b`, `err-dns-*`, `err-tls-*`, `err-db-*`, `err-old-*`) | `error-conn-<class>-title` / `-body`. The class ID has `.` and `_` turned into `-`, e.g. `error-conn-auth-invalid-title` | `hacivat-core.ftl` (dynamic family `error-conn-*`) |
  | `ssh-*`, `hk-*`, `plain-*`, the TLS certificate dialog (`tls-title` … `tls-copy-fp`, `tls-pin*`, `tls-remove-pin`), `lost-*`, `cap-reconnecting`, `cap-next` | **not added in M2a** (M2b) | — |
  | `unit-s`, `toast-connected` | **not added**: no number is joined to a unit in code (P28), and a successful connect is shown by the capsule, not a toast (P42) | — |

  - `prod-save` (Turkish `{Env} olarak kaydet`) uses a Fluent select on `$env`, documented as `$env (Enum(production, staging, test, dev)) - the chosen tag` (the schema grammar; `(Enum: a|b)` is L006 `BadVariableDoc`, P27). Each branch carries its own capitalised word, so nothing is cased in code.
  - Every timed string is one message with the unit inside it (INV-1, P28): `hv-conn-test-ok` (`$ms (Number)`, "Connected in { $ms } ms"), `hv-conn-test-clock` and `hv-capsule-clock` (`$seconds (Decimal)`, `{ NUMBER($seconds, minimumFractionDigits: 2) } s`, TR `… sn`), and `hv-conn-err-elapsed` (`minimumFractionDigits: 3`).
  - In `error-conn-*` messages, `$port` and `$version` are `(String)`, never `(Number)`: a port never gets grouping separators (`5.432`) (P20).
  - `connections.toml` diagnostics are shown, never dropped (P29): `hv-conn-file-quarantined` (`$file`), `hv-conn-file-entries-skipped` (`$count (Number)`), `hv-conn-file-password-ignored` (never echoes the value) and `hv-conn-store-read-only`, all owned by Task 12.
  - `locales/tr/hacivat-core.ftl` does not exist yet; Task 10 creates it (header plus its block).
- **Engines and versions (C10, PO Q3).**
  - Floors: PostgreSQL 14, MySQL 8.0, MariaDB 10.6.
  - Tested: PostgreSQL 14–18; MySQL 8.0 and 8.4; MariaDB 10.6, 10.11 and 11.4.
  - SQLite uses the bundled engine. An engine with no `floor` (SQLite) is always `Supported` (P4).
  - Below the floor: `server.unsupported_version`. Above the floor but untested: connect, with a `NotTested` warning.
  - MySQL and MariaDB `VerifyCa` is unsupported in M2a (P11): Validate rejects it with `IssueCode::TlsModeUnsupported`, the TLS Select disables it, and ADR 0002 records the `mysql_async` defect.
- **Docker.**
  - Every test that needs a server is marked `#[ignore = "docker"]`. Such tests never run in a plain `cargo test`.
  - They run through `cargo xtask docker test --set pr|full` (Task 11), which checks that every selected service is healthy and runs them with `--ignored`, `HACIVAT_DOCKER=1` and `HACIVAT_DOCKER_ARTIFACTS`.
  - Server targets are read only from `docker/m2a/targets.toml`, through `hacivat-testkit`.
  - The compose project is `name: hacivat-m2a`, and every service has `container_name: m2a-<id>` (P25).
  - **Only the controller runs `cargo xtask docker up` or `down`, and keeps the project running through W3 and W4.** An agent never runs `up` or `down` unless its task text says so (Task 11 Step 4 is the only one). `xtask docker test` never runs `up`: it fails fast and names the unhealthy services, instead of recreating containers from another worktree's bind mounts.
  - Files that Docker produces (`mysql84-ca.pem`) are reached only through `hacivat_testkit::artifact(name)`, which reads `HACIVAT_DOCKER_ARTIFACTS` (set by `xtask docker` to `<main checkout>/target/docker`). A path relative to the test's working directory is never used.
  - No test calls `std::env::set_var` or `remove_var`: both are `unsafe` in edition 2024, and the workspace denies `unsafe_code`. The environment is read through pure functions instead, e.g. `docker_enabled_from(Option<&str>)`.
- **Full test set.** "All tests pass" means every line below. Each task runs the lines for the crates it touches. PR CI runs lines 1–5 (Task 11 adds line 5 as a step of the `docker-pr` job), nightly CI runs line 6, and `docker-pr` runs line 7 with `--set pr`. The gate runs all of them.
  ```
  cargo test --workspace
  cargo test -p hacivat-ui-core --features test-support,scene-check
  cargo test -p hacivat-l10n --features pseudo
  cargo test -p hacivat-app --features scene-check,pseudo
  cargo test -p hacivat-driver --features fake
  cargo test --release -p hacivat-l10n --features pseudo
  cargo xtask docker test --set full            # Docker (local; CI runs --set pr)
  HACIVAT_KEYRING_LIVE=1 cargo test -p hacivat-secrets -- --ignored live_keyring_round_trip   # gate, PO's Mac
  ```
  Every `hacivat-driver` test target that uses `FakeDriver` is declared with `[[test]] required-features = ["fake"]`, so a plain `cargo test -p hacivat-driver` still compiles and line 5 runs them (P45).
- **Execution (L1 and M1 lessons).** These are hard rules:
  - **Branches.** Feature branch `m2a`. Each task works in its own worktree. Worktrees are created from `main` (M1 lesson), so the agent's first commands are `git checkout -B m2a/<task-slug> m2a` and `git merge-base --is-ancestor m2a HEAD`. Stop if either fails.
  - **SDD files (P24).** The ledger, `global-constraints.md` and the implementer and reviewer rules live in `<main checkout>/.superpowers/sdd/2026-11-12-m2a-connect/`. `.superpowers/` is git-ignored, so worktrees never contain it; every brief cites these files by absolute main-checkout path.
  - **Target directories (M1 lesson on shared target-dir collisions).**
    - Tasks that build `gpui` (every `crates/ui`/`crates/app` task, and the gate) share `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=<main checkout>/target`. Core-only tasks share `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=<main checkout>/target-core`.
    - Workspace path crates get the same build hash in every worktree, so tasks that build the *same* crate at the *same* time get their own directory, `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=<main checkout>/target-core/<task>` (P14):
      - **W1:** T2 and T3 run as **one agent, in order** (the same crate, `hacivat-driver`). That agent (`target-core/t02-t03`), T4 (`target-core/t04`) and T5 (`target-core/t05`) each use their own directory, because T2 and T3 compile T5's `hacivat-net`.
      - **W3:** T13, T14, T15 and T16 each use `target-core/<task>`, because T16 compiles the stub driver crates while T13–T15 compile the real ones.
      - **W4:** `PreparedQuery` belongs to T10 (W2), so no W4 task edits `hacivat-l10n`, and the shared gpui `target` stays safe.
      - The controller deletes the per-task directories after each wave.
    - Before its final verification, a task runs `cargo clean -p <crates it touched>`.
    - Nobody but the controller runs `cargo test --workspace` while other agents are building.
  - **Append-only shared files.** Each task appends a block and never edits another block. The heading depends on the file (P41):
    - FTL and TOML: `## m2a-tNN-<slug>`;
    - Rust files: `// ## m2a-tNN-<slug>`;
    - ADR 0002: `## m2a-tNN — <crate>`;
    - `ci.yml`: `# ## m2a-tNN-<slug>`.

    The files:
    - `locales/{en,tr}/{hacivat-ui,hacivat-core}.ftl`
    - `locales/_schema/literal-allowlist.toml`
    - the `xtask/src/main.rs` dispatch arms
    - the registration block in `hacivat_app::init`
    - the registration block in `hacivat_app::connections::install` (T17, T18 and T20 each append one line)
    - `docs/decisions/0002-driver-crates.md` (one section per driver task)
    - the jobs in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`
    - helper functions in `crates/app/hacivat-app/tests/support/mod.rs`
    - `crates/ui/hacivat-ui-core/assets/icons/lucide/NOTICE.md` (the icon list)

    The controller resolves append-only conflicts by keeping both blocks. Resolve a `Cargo.lock` conflict by taking the branch tip and running `cargo check --workspace --locked`.
  - **Modal layers (M1 strong-entity fix).** A custom modal view pushed with `Workspace::push_layer` subscribes to its own events *before* the push, and keeps a strong clone until an event has been delivered (the `open_dialog` pattern).
  - **Escape and paste in fields (P16).** A focused `TextInput` swallows Escape: it emits `InputEvent::Cancelled` and stops propagation. Every custom layer subscribes to `InputEvent::Cancelled` on each of its inputs and handles it as its own Escape. A paste reaches the app only through `TextInput::set_paste_filter` (Task 6), never by re-implementing paste; masked inputs never call the filter.
  - **Key bindings.** `menus::rebuild` owns GPUI key bindings (M1); nothing else calls `cx.bind_keys`. While a modal layer is open, only bindings of that layer's context plus `app.quit` resolve (P47).
  - **Writes.** Settings writes go through `shell::update_settings`. Profile writes go only through `ConnectionsModel`. Each has a single ordered writer.
  - **Rate limits.** Watch `~/.claude/rate-limits.json`. Pause new dispatches at `five_hour ≥ 80 %` or `seven_day ≥ 90 %`.
  - **PO away.** When a decision comes up while the PO is away, log it in the ledger as `Ruling (PO-away)`. The departures from spec L15's M2a list (R3, R5, R8, R12) and the L15(b) mock sign-off are logged there (P26); E7 at the gate still needs the PO's real sign-off.

## Review Focus

1. **Pasted and copied connection strings carrying secrets.** Users paste many kinds of strings:
   - URLs whose password contains `@ : / %`;
   - IPv6 hosts and non-ASCII database names;
   - `jdbc:` prefixes, surrounding whitespace and newlines;
   - input over 64 KiB;
   - a password that happens to look like a URL, pasted into the Password field.

   The expected behaviour: the password is split off and is never written to `connections.toml`, logs or the clipboard. Copy as URL never contains it. Pasting into the Password field inserts the text literally.

   Tests: `display_and_copy_never_contain_the_password` (Task 3), `url_paste_edge_vectors` (Task 7), `paste_into_password_field_is_literal` (Task 17), `connections_toml_never_contains_a_secret` (Task 12), `connect_logs_never_contain_the_password` (Task 16).
2. **Hand-edited or foreign `connections.toml`.** Expect duplicate or missing ids, an unknown engine, a `password = "…"` key that a user typed in, a newer format version, unknown keys, or text that does not parse. The app starts with what it can read. It quarantines an unparsable file. It never echoes or re-writes a typed password. It keeps unknown keys. It refuses to write a newer major version.

   Tests in Task 12: `password_key_typed_into_file_is_ignored_and_reported`, `unknown_keys_are_preserved_on_save`, `newer_version_is_read_only`, `unparsable_file_is_quarantined`, `duplicate_ids_keep_the_first`, `unknown_engine_entry_skipped`; and `boot_reports_store_diagnostics_once` (P29).
3. **Turkish input and Turkish servers.**
   - Host and profile names with `İ`/`ı`.
   - Option keys such as `applİcation_name`.
   - A PostgreSQL server that answers `ÖLÜMCÜL … parola doğrulaması başarısız`.

   Classification follows the code; matching uses ASCII folding only; and production suggestion matches whole ASCII tokens `prod|prd|live|production` only (split on `[-._/:0-9]`, P7), so `delivery-db` is not suggested.

   Tests: `code_beats_localized_text` (Task 10), `option_keys_fold_ascii_only` (Task 2), `dsn_keys_fold_ascii_only` (Task 7), `prod_suggestion_ascii_only` and `turkish_names_are_ascii_folded_only` (Task 12), `non_ascii_database_survives_percent_decoding` (Task 7), `turkish_server_wrong_password_is_auth_invalid` (Task 19).
4. **The keychain is unavailable, denied, slow or too small.** Cases: the macOS Keychain is locked and the user clicks Deny; Linux has no Secret Service; a Windows blob is over 2,560 bytes; a keychain dialog takes 20 s. The app never blocks the UI thread. It falls back to keeping the secret for this session, with one honest toast. It never writes plaintext. A keychain prompt does not count toward the connect timeout until it returns (HV-CONN-01 edge case).

   Tests: `keyring_denied_falls_back_to_session_with_reason`, `windows_blob_limit_falls_back` and `keyring_backend_is_native_not_mock` (Task 4); `slow_prompt_does_not_count_toward_timeout` and `slow_keyring_does_not_count_toward_timeout` (Task 16, P23); `secret_ops_never_run_on_the_ui_thread` and `keyring_fallback_toast_once_per_session` (Task 12).
5. **Connect-lifecycle races.**
   - Esc while connecting; closing the dialog while a test runs.
   - Double-click connect.
   - Deleting or editing a profile while it connects.
   - Quitting during a connect.
   - A driver that completes after the timeout.

   The expected behaviour: one attempt per profile (single-flight); cleanup runs LIFO; late connections are closed; a deleted profile's session secrets are forgotten; quitting closes everything within 2 s (the quit handler blocks on `shutdown_blocking`, because GPUI waits only 200 ms for quit futures, P15).

   Tests: `cancel_mid_connect_runs_cleanups_lifo`, `concurrent_connects_single_flight`, `late_connection_after_timeout_is_closed`, `shutdown_closes_all_within_budget` (Task 16); `delete_while_connecting_cancels_and_forgets_secrets`, `editing_a_connecting_profile_cancels_then_saves` and `quit_during_connect_closes_the_late_connection` (Task 18, P39); `closing_dialog_cancels_running_test` (Task 21).

## Assumptions and rulings

These are controller calls made while the PO is away, and the PO can overrule each one. Each says why, and what it costs if it is wrong.

- **R1. Crate set and placement.**
  - New crates: `hacivat-proto`, `hacivat-net`, `hacivat-secrets`, `hacivat-driver`, `hacivat-driver-{pg,mysql,sqlite}`, `hacivat-session`, and `hacivat-testkit` (dev-only).
  - The `hacivat-core::core::*` paths in `alg/01` map to the canonical §5.1 crates:
    - `caps`, `driver`, `url`, `sanitize`, `errors` → `hacivat-driver`;
    - `conn::pipeline`, `conn::test`, `conn::singleflight`, `session::manager` → `hacivat-session`;
    - `secrets::{store,files,providers,export}` → `hacivat-secrets`;
    - `net::{tls,direct,forwarder}` → `hacivat-net`.
  - There is no `hacivat-core` facade yet (§8 item 4). There is no `hacivat-guard` and no `hacivat-values` until M2b/M3.
  - *Cost if wrong:* moving modules later.
- **R2. ID numbering.** L15 names "TLS Auto flows (HV-SEC-01 …)" and "SSH (HV-SEC-02)", but `alg/01` numbers SSH as SEC-01 and TLS as SEC-02 (Register Appendix B #10 already flags these cross-references). This plan follows `alg/01`'s numbers and L15's content.
- **R3. TLS in M2a is the non-interactive half of D-005 (HV-SEC-02).**
  - **M2a has:** modes Disable/Auto/Require/VerifyCa/VerifyFull, a CA file, a client certificate with an unencrypted PEM key, the platform verifier, TLS 1.2 minimum, and the permanent "unencrypted" badge.
  - **`Auto`:** verify whenever the server offers TLS. Plaintext is allowed only for loopback, RFC 1918/4193 private addresses and Unix sockets, and then the badge shows. A public host without TLS fails with `tls.not_supported_by_server` **at the TLS step, before any credential is sent** (P9: PostgreSQL uses `SslMode::Require` when plaintext is not allowed; the PostConnect check stays as a second line of defence). An untrusted chain fails with `tls.untrusted`, and a name mismatch with `tls.hostname`.
  - **Interim quick fix:** "Encrypt without verifying" (`FixId::EncryptWithoutVerifying`, string `hv-conn-fix-require`) sets the profile's mode to Require and connects. It is honest, and the user chooses it. The capsule and the sheet then say `TLS 1.3 · not verified` (`hv-conn-enc-unverified`, new string, PO review).
  - **M2b:** pinning, CA import from the prompt, and the "public host without TLS" ask dialog. M2b re-evaluates the interim fix.
  - *Why:* Docker MySQL 8.x and MariaDB 11.4 ship self-signed certificates, so without this fix M2a could not reach a default local MySQL in `Auto`.
  - *Cost if wrong:* one fix id and one string removed in M2b.
- **R4. No UI shells for the TLS certificate dialog or the SSH settings in M2a.**
  - The connection dialog has no SSH disclosure. The `network.dns` error offers no "Connect through SSH…".
  - `TransportSpec::SshTunnel` is reserved; Validate rejects it with `config.invalid` / `IssueCode::TransportUnavailable`.
  - *Why:* "Trust is visible". A control that does nothing misleads, and a shell would be tested twice. The disclosure list is data, so M2b adds the designed rows without changes elsewhere.
  - *Cost:* none beyond M2b's planned work.
- **R5. The Linux master-password vault goes to M2b, not M9.**
  - M2a ships the `VaultBackend` trait only.
  - On Linux without Secret Service, "Forever" falls back to "Until restart" with one `hv-conn-keyring-unavailable` toast. The Remember Select shows Forever as disabled, with a hint.
  - *Why M2b:* the unlock prompt reuses the `Interaction` plumbing that M2b extends for SSH passphrases, host keys and TLS pins. Linux users start daily use in M3. HV-SEC-05's "vault on CI Linux" test belongs with secrets, not with release hardening.
  - *Cost:* until M2b, Linux users without a keyring retype passwords after a restart.
- **R6. Linux keyring.** Use `keyring` with `async-secret-service` + `crypto-rust` + `tokio`: pure-Rust zbus, no libdbus. Task 4 verifies the Linux build in CI. Linux keyring round trips are manual (no D-Bus session in CI).
- **R7. Keyring entries and dev signing.**
  - The service is `hacivat/<profile-uuid>/<slot>`, or `hacivat-dev/<profile-uuid>/<slot>` when `cfg(debug_assertions)`. The account is `hacivat`.
  - Slots: `db-password`, `tls-key-passphrase`. `ssh-<hop>-password`, `ssh-<hop>-passphrase` and `proxy-password` are reserved.
  - Dev builds avoid repeated macOS Keychain prompts by signing with a stable local identity:
    - `scripts/dev-sign.sh --create-identity` makes a self-signed "Hacivat Dev" code-signing identity in the login keychain, once.
    - `scripts/dev-run.sh` (the macOS cargo `runner` in `.cargo/config.toml`) signs the binary with `--identifier dev.hacivat.app` and runs it.
    - Without the identity, or whenever `codesign` fails (for example an untrusted certificate), it prints one hint on stderr and runs the binary unsigned. It never fails (P35).
  - Windows Credential Manager blobs over 2,560 bytes raise `SecretError::TooLarge`, and the store falls back to UntilRestart.
- **R8. Retention.**
  - The default is Forever (PO Q4).
  - *Save* stores the draft password according to its retention: Forever → keyring, UntilRestart → memory, ForSession → held for the next session, Never → dropped.
  - Test connection never stores anything.
  - A password re-entered after `auth.invalid` is stored only after a successful connect (HV-SEC-03 step 4).
  - One budget of 3 server rejections per connect gesture (P8): a stored or draft password that fails is not re-prompted by the pipeline; the error dialog's re-entry continues the same count.
  - Lowering the retention deletes the keyring entry at save.
- **R9. One profiles file.**
  - `connections.toml` (§5.6), `version = 1`, UUID ids. There is no `connections.local.toml` split; portable export is v1.x (C16).
  - Unknown keys are preserved (toml_edit). A newer major version makes the store read-only, with no writes and a toast. An unparsable file is quarantined like settings.
- **R10. Read-only in M2a is a label plus a minimal session setting.**
  - The sheet and the capsule tooltip say "read-only (guarded)".
  - PostgreSQL gets `-c default_transaction_read_only=on`. MySQL/MariaDB get `SET SESSION TRANSACTION READ ONLY` at PostConnect. SQLite opens with `SQLITE_OPEN_READONLY`.
  - `AccessLabel::ReadOnlyEnforced` exists but is never produced.
  - `hacivat-guard`, the role probe and the "enforced" label come in M2b.
  - MariaDB stays "guarded" (C19).
- **R11. Unsupported servers (PO Q3).**
  - Below the floor, the result is `server.unsupported_version` at CheckServer, with fixes `[SupportedVersions, EditConnection, ConnectAnyway]`.
  - ConnectAnyway persists `allow_unsupported_server = true` on the profile. From then on, the capsule, status bar and sheet carry an "unsupported" mark (`hv-capsule-unsupported`, new string) for as long as the server is below the floor.
  - "Supported versions" opens `https://hacivat.dev/docs/engines`. This is a placeholder URL and an open item for the PO.
- **R12. HV-CONN scope.**
  - CONN-01, -03 and -07 ship in full.
  - CONN-04: `RawConn::ping`, plus a fixed liveness ping (`ManagerConfig::liveness`, 30 s) that marks a live connection `Lost`. The capsule then shows Failed with Retry. The keep-alive interval is stored and shown. The supervisor tick, sleep/wake handling and idle disconnect come in M2b.
  - CONN-05 (reconnect, backoff, state replay, and the D-085 "session lost" toast) comes in M2b.
  - CONN-02 comes in M4, with its first consumer, per-database introspection. "Use <db>" edits the profile's database instead.
- **R13. Budget seam (D-035).**
  - `hacivat-session::budget::Budget`: 4 for remote engines and 1 for SQLite.
  - The main connection takes one permit. Test connections take none, because they use fresh resources (CONN-07).
  - The SessionManager in M2b consumes it. Advanced states it as a fact ("Up to 4 open at once").
- **R14. Interaction.**
  - `UiPort::ask(Question) -> Answer` is async.
  - M2a asks only `Question::Password`, through a 480 px password dialog that reuses the error dialog's re-entry field.
  - In `Headless` mode, a missing secret fails with `ConnectError::needs_interaction = true` and no prompt.
- **R15. l10n carry-overs from the M1 ledger.**
  - A new documented variable type `(Decimal)` generates an `ExactDecimal` field, for durations such as `0,06 sn`. `(Number)` stays `Count`, so M1 call sites don't churn. The settings preview drops `t_dynamic` for the typed ID.
  - `PreparedQuery` (the search key of a query, built once) lands in Task 10 (W2), so that no W4 task edits `hacivat-l10n` (P14). Task 18's explorer filter consumes it.
  - Any `WhenValue::Instant` goes through the `jiff` path. M2a formats no instants.
  - L026 (registry codes without English messages) is implemented over `errors.toml` (Task 10).
- **R16. Duration copy.**
  - Totals and pings are whole milliseconds: `Connected in 54 ms`.
  - Running clocks show seconds with 2 decimals; the technical-details "Elapsed" has 3.
  - Every timed string is one message with the unit inside it (INV-1, P28): `hv-conn-test-ok` (`$ms (Number)`), `hv-conn-test-clock` and `hv-capsule-clock` (`$seconds (Decimal)`, `{ NUMBER($seconds, minimumFractionDigits: 2) } s` / TR `sn`), `hv-conn-err-elapsed` (`minimumFractionDigits: 3`). There is no `hv-conn-unit-s`.
- **R17. Docker harness.**
  - `docker/m2a/compose.yml` and `docker/m2a/targets.toml`, with the services listed in Task 11.
  - Images use major tags. The resolved versions are recorded at the gate.
  - **Minimal CI job in M2a:** `docker-pr` on ubuntu, with `pg17` and `mysql84` (SQLite needs no service). The full matrix in CI comes in M2b (L15).
- **R18. Driver ADR and the MySQL fallbacks.** `docs/decisions/0002-driver-crates.md` gets one section per driver task, covering four points: stream injection, the TLS hook, the cancel side channel, and the licence tree.
  - **`mysql_async` 0.37 cannot run over an injected stream** (verified in its source, P10: `Conn::new` always dials itself). Non-Direct transports (M2b), and any connect with a `transport_wrap` set (P13), therefore go through `LoopbackForwarder`. For `mysql_async` the forwarder is always TCP `127.0.0.1:0`, on every OS, with `max_accepts = 1`, because `mysql_async` never applies TLS over a Unix socket. `LoopbackForwarder`'s Unix mode stays for drivers that can use it.
  - Every MySQL connection sets `prefer_socket(false)`, so `mysql_async` never reconnects through `@@socket` after the handshake (that would bypass the forwarder and TLS). TLS through the forwarder verifies the real name through `SslOpts::with_danger_tls_hostname_override(tls_host)`.
  - **Direct transport** uses `mysql_async`'s own TCP connection: `ip_or_hostname(tls_host)` plus `resolved_ips(<the pipeline's resolved addresses>)`, so TLS hostname checks see the real name.
  - **Trust (ledger ruling, PO-away):** `SslOpts::with_disable_built_in_roots(true)`, with the OS roots from `rustls-native-certs` and the CA file passed as DER through `with_root_certs`. `webpki-roots` is still compiled in, because `mysql_async`'s `rustls-tls` feature always pulls it; Task 1 adds the scoped `deny.toml` exception (CDLA-Permissive-2.0) and Task 14 records it in ADR 0002 (P1). If the PO rejects that, the fallback is `native-tls`.
- **R19. The capsule follows `ConnectionsModel::current()`.** That is the explorer-selected profile when it has a live state, otherwise the most recent connect attempt. There are no query tabs before M3, so "Status Follows The Tab" starts in M3.
- **R20. Keys and menus.**
  - The explorer list registers the key context `Explorer` through `shell::register_focus_context(cx, handle, "Explorer")` (a `FocusContexts` Global, P4). It applies only when the list itself has focus, not the filter field.
  - Task 1 owns all keymap data edits.
  - Esc-to-cancel-connect is a shell hook (`ShellHooks::escape`), not a binding, so Esc still pops layers. The explorer filter's `InputEvent::Cancelled` also invokes the hook (P16).
  - Only `connection.new` goes into the native menu bar, in a new File group (`hv-menu-file`) that Task 1 adds to `menus::layout()` (P18). M1's native menu snapshots enablement (M1 T16 note #3), and the other connection actions are conditional.
  - While a modal layer is open, only bindings of that layer's context plus `app.quit` resolve, so `cmd-n` cannot stack a second dialog (P47).
- **R21. Paste interception (mock `looksLikeConn`).**
  - Every field receives pastes through `TextInput::set_paste_filter` (Task 6, P16); a filter that returns `true` consumes the paste.
  - The URL field parses every paste.
  - Other text fields parse a paste only when `looks_like_connection` matches it. Its regexes are `^\s*(jdbc:)?(postgres|postgresql|mysql|mariadb)://` (ASCII case-insensitive), `^\s*file:`, and `\b(host|dbname)\s*=\s*\S`.
  - The Password field never intercepts: masked inputs never call the paste filter, so the rule holds in the widget itself.
  - A multi-host URL uses the first host, and the notice says so (`hv-conn-url-multihost`, new string).
- **R22. Naming and environment rules.**
  - The suggested name is the mock's `suggestName` (`index.html:903`).
  - The production suggestion (PO Q5 as amended by the ledger ruling, PO-away; P7) splits the host, then the name, into tokens on `[-._/:0-9]` and compares whole tokens, ASCII-case-insensitively, with `prod`, `prd`, `live` and `production` (`production` reports `"prod"`; one list to change). The first match wins, host before name. `production_hint` returns `Option<(&'static str, HintSource)>` with `HintSource { Host, Name }`, so the prompt says "the host name contains" or "the name contains" (`hv-conn-prod-suggest`, `hv-conn-prod-suggest-name`). `delivery-db` no longer matches; `pg.production.acme.io` and `prod1.acme.io` do.
  - The environment is set to local automatically for `localhost` (ASCII case-insensitive, trailing dot allowed), `127.0.0.0/8`, `::1`, Unix sockets and SQLite.
  - RFC 1918 hosts are **not** local, so the user is asked.
  - The prompt appears on every save of a profile with no environment and a non-local endpoint.
- **R23. Duplicate.** The new name is `<name>-2` (then `-3`, … at the first free number). Stored secrets are copied to the new id.
- **R24. pg_service import.**
  - A 480 px dialog lists the services, each with a checkbox and an Environment Select. Production is preselected under R22.
  - Imported profiles have `Origin::Imported { source: PgService }` and go through DRV-05. A `password` from the file goes to the keyring with retention Forever.
  - Import stays disabled until every selected remote row has an environment.
- **R25. Where a failure shows in the trail** (derived from the error class family):
  - `network.dns` → Resolve; other `network.*` → Connect;
  - `tls.*` → TLS;
  - `auth.*`, `authz.*`, `config.unknown_database` and the other `server.*` → Sign in;
  - `server.unsupported_version` → Check server;
  - SQLite classes → Open file;
  - `cancelled` and `internal` → the step that was running;
  - `config.invalid` → no trail; the issues show inline instead.
- **R26. English copies.** "Copy report" and "Copy details (English)" are rendered in the app from the `en` bundle. Core never formats English (§3.4).
- **R27. The title bar during a modal.** It stays visible: the scrim starts at y = 40 px (PO Q1). Its controls are inert (`TitleBar::inert`), while window dragging and the traffic lights still work.
- **R28. Shots data.** The harness drives every surface through public `*_fixture` constructors behind the app's `shots` feature. It needs no database. Each fixture is written by the task that owns its surface (T9, T17, T18, T20; P19), takes `(window, cx)` and opens its surface over a `ConnectionsBoot::for_tests` shell.
- **R29. Driver trait shape.** It uses `BoxFuture` (no `async-trait` macro). The M2a `RawConn` surface is probes only; M3 adds execute and fetch.
- **R30. Explicitly not in M2a:**
  - the session actor, transactions, and cancelling a running statement;
  - guard layers and the role probe;
  - reconnect and session replay;
  - SSH, interactive TLS, the vault, `my.cnf`, and opt-in env-variable secret references;
  - the keep-alive supervisor;
  - CONN-02;
  - importing from other tools, and the query console (L15 c).
- **R31. Explorer group for profiles without an environment.** It goes last, as "No environment" (`hv-explorer-group-unset`, new string). This only happens with hand-edited files.

## Waves

The work is interface-first. Task 1 commits:
- every cross-task type and signature in this plan, with non-panicking stubs (F31);
- working minimal versions of the helpers that a same-wave task consumes (F4): `StreamFactory`, `CleanupGuard`, `MemoryBackend`, `FakeDriver`, `CoreRuntime`, `install_crypto_provider` and the `connections::boot` wiring;
- every new manifest, with complete dependencies (the Step 3 table), and `deny.toml`;
- the shell hook slots;
- all keymap data and the native-menu File group;
- the icon assets.

| Wave | Runs | Tasks (parallel agents) | Unblocked by |
|---|---|---|---|
| W0 | serial | Task 1 | — |
| W1 | 4 | T2→T3 driver core then URL templates (one agent, in order, P14) · T4 secret store · T5 net · T6 UI form primitives | T1 merged |
| W2 | 5 | T7 URL parse (needs T3) · T8 credential files and providers (needs T4) · T9 UI status primitives · T10 error taxonomy and l10n carry-overs (incl. `PreparedQuery`) · T11 Docker harness | W1 merged |
| W3 | 5 | T12 profiles and app model (T3, T7, T4) · T13 PostgreSQL driver (T2, T5, T11) · T14 MySQL/MariaDB driver (T2, T5, T11) · T15 SQLite driver (T2, T11) · T16 pipeline, test, manager (T2, T4, T5, T8, T10) | W2 merged |
| W4 | 4 | T17 connection dialog (T6, T7, T12) · T18 explorer, sheet, capsule, status, commands (T9, T12, T16) · T19 engine conformance suite (T13–T16) · T20 test strip, errors, prompts (T9, T10, T16) | W3 merged |
| W5 | serial | T21 shots, cross-surface tests, DESIGN.md | W4 merged |
| W6 | serial | T22 gate | everything |

## File Structure

```
hacivat/
  Cargo.toml                       # + workspace deps (Tech Stack) and the nine new crates as workspace deps
  .cargo/config.toml               # + macOS runner scripts/dev-run.sh (T4; the existing xtask alias stays)
  deny.toml                        # + webpki-roots licence exception, aws-lc bans (T1)
  scripts/{dev-sign.sh, dev-run.sh}
  docker/m2a/{compose.yml, targets.toml, pg-tls/init.sh, pg-tr/Dockerfile, init/{pg.sql, mysql.sql}, pgpass-vectors/}
  docs/decisions/0002-driver-crates.md
  crates/core/
    hacivat-proto/src/lib.rs                       # DbmsKind, ProfileId, Department, EnvTag, Origin, ErrClass, FixId, AccessLabel
    hacivat-net/src/{lib, stream, direct, forwarder, address, cleanup, tls}.rs
    hacivat-secrets/src/{lib, store, scrub, providers}.rs, backend/{mod, memory, keyring, vault}.rs, files/{mod, pgpass, pg_service}.rs
```

**Interfaces (Produces; every later task consumes these names).** Every data type derives `Debug, Clone, PartialEq`, and also `Eq, Hash` where the fields allow, `Copy` for fieldless enums, and `Default` where a default is named. Types holding a `SecretString` derive `Debug` only (secrecy redacts), never `PartialEq`; tests compare them with `matches!` or accessors (P6). `BoxFuture` is `futures::future::BoxFuture`. Stubs follow F31: `Err(…Unsupported)` for fallible async functions, empty collections, `None`, `false`, or no-ops. Stub parameters are `_`-prefixed, and placeholder private fields carry `#[allow(dead_code, reason = "Task N")]`, so `clippy -D warnings` stays clean (P3).

- **`hacivat-proto`.** Complete in Task 1, with no stubs.
  - **`DbmsKind`.** Variants: `Postgres, Redshift, Cockroach, Yugabyte, Timescale, MySql, MariaDb, TiDb, MsSql, AzureSql, Sqlite, ClickHouse, Redis, Valkey, MongoDb, DocumentDb, Oracle, Unknown`.
    - `id() -> &'static str`: `postgres`, `redshift`, `cockroach`, `yugabyte`, `timescale`, `mysql`, `mariadb`, `tidb`, `mssql`, `azure_sql`, `sqlite`, `clickhouse`, `redis`, `valkey`, `mongodb`, `documentdb`, `oracle`, `unknown`.
    - `from_id(&str) -> Option<Self>`.
    - `display_name() -> &'static str`: product names, never translated (`PostgreSQL`, `MySQL`, `MariaDB`, `SQLite`, …).
  - **`ProfileId(pub Uuid)`.** `new() -> Self` (v4). `Display` is lowercase hyphenated. Also `FromStr`, and serde as a string.
  - **`Department`.** The `alg/01` §0.4 list.
  - **`EnvTag { Local, Dev, Test, Staging, Production }`.** `id()` (`local` … `production`), `from_id`, and `severity_rank() -> u8` (Production 0, Staging 1, Test 2, Dev 3, Local 4).
  - **`TagSource { User, Org }`.**
  - **`Origin`** = `UserTyped | Imported { source: ImportSource } | PerdeManaged { org: String } | DeepLink | Mcp`, with `ImportSource { PgService, Url }`.
  - **`ErrClass`.** One variant per ID below, in this order. `ErrClass::ALL: &[ErrClass]`.
    - `network.dns`, `network.refused`, `network.timeout`, `network.reset`, `network.unreachable`, `network.ipv6_unavailable`
    - `tls.handshake`, `tls.untrusted`, `tls.hostname`, `tls.protocol`, `tls.client_cert_required`, `tls.not_supported_by_server`
    - `ssh.host_key_unknown`, `ssh.host_key_changed`, `ssh.auth`, `ssh.channel_open`, `ssh.agent_unavailable`
    - `auth.invalid`, `auth.expired`, `auth.expiring`, `auth.locked`, `auth.method_unsupported`, `auth.mfa_required`
    - `authz.database`, `authz.object`
    - `config.invalid` (NEW), `config.unknown_database`, `config.mode_mismatch`, `config.wrong_port_protocol`, `config.pooler_prepared_statements`
    - `server.too_many_connections`, `server.starting`, `server.shutdown`, `server.read_only`, `server.unsupported_version` (NEW)
    - `karagoz.forbidden`, `karagoz.grant_expired`, `karagoz.revoked`, `karagoz.upstream_unreachable`, `karagoz.upstream_auth`, `karagoz.overloaded`, `karagoz.protocol`, `karagoz.session_timeout`, `karagoz.resume_expired`, `karagoz.direct_path_unavailable`, `karagoz.peer_identity_mismatch`
    - `cancelled`, `internal`

    Methods:
    - `id() -> &'static str` and `from_id(&str)`;
    - `fluent_stem() -> String`: `"error-conn-"` + the ID with `.` and `_` turned into `-`;
    - `family() -> ErrFamily { Network, Tls, Ssh, Auth, Authz, Config, Server, Karagoz, Cancelled, Internal }`;
    - `retryable_on_reconnect() -> bool`: true only for `network.{timeout,reset,refused}`, `server.starting`, `karagoz.overloaded` and `karagoz.upstream_unreachable`.
  - **`FixId`.** `EditConnection, TryAgain, ReenterPassword, UseDatabase, SupportedVersions, ConnectAnyway, EncryptWithoutVerifying, ReviewCertificate, ImportCa, ConnectThroughSsh, ChangePassword, UseSingleSessionMode, LowerConnectionBudget, SwitchToCluster, SwitchToStandalone, DisableServerPreparedStatements, ReviewHostKey`, with `id()` in snake_case.
  - **`AccessLabel { ReadWrite, ReadOnlyGuarded, ReadOnlyEnforced }`.**

  - **`forwarder`.**
    - `pub enum ForwarderAddr { Tcp(SocketAddr), Unix(PathBuf) }`
    - `pub enum ForwarderMode { Tcp, UnixOrTcp }`: `Tcp` always binds `127.0.0.1:0` (every OS; the MySQL path, P10); `UnixOrTcp` binds a `0600` Unix socket in a `0700` directory on Unix and `127.0.0.1:0` on Windows.
    - `pub struct LoopbackForwarder`, with `bind(factory: StreamFactory, private_dir: &Path, max_accepts: u32, mode: ForwarderMode) -> BoxFuture<'static, io::Result<(LoopbackForwarder, CleanupGuard)>>`, `addr(&self) -> &ForwarderAddr` and `accepted(&self) -> u64`.

    - `pub struct TlsSettings { pub mode: TlsMode, pub ca_file: Option<PathBuf>, pub client_cert: Option<ClientCertPaths> }`
    - `pub enum TlsFailure { UnknownIssuer, NameMismatch, Expired, NotYetValid, Protocol, ClientCertRequired, Other }`
    - `pub struct TlsParams { pub settings: TlsSettings, pub config: Arc<rustls::ClientConfig>, pub server_name: rustls::pki_types::ServerName<'static>, pub plaintext_allowed: bool, pub failure: Arc<Mutex<Option<TlsFailure>>> }`
    - `pub enum TlsVerified { Full, CaOnly, No }`
    - `pub struct TlsSummary { pub encrypted: bool, pub version: Option<String>, pub cipher: Option<String>, pub verified: TlsVerified }`
    - `pub enum TlsSetupError { CaFileUnreadable(PathBuf), CertFileUnreadable(PathBuf), KeyUnreadable(PathBuf), KeyEncrypted(PathBuf) }`
    - `pub fn build_tls_params(settings: &TlsSettings, tls_host: &str, class: AddressClass) -> Result<Option<TlsParams>, TlsSetupError>`. `None` means Disable.
    - `pub fn install_crypto_provider()` (*working*, P5): calls `rustls::crypto::ring::default_provider().install_default()` and ignores "already installed". `CoreRuntime::new` calls it; tests that build TLS configs call it first.

    - `pub trait SecretBackend: Send + Sync + 'static`. Its methods are blocking: `get(&self, key: &SecretKey) -> Result<Option<SecretString>, SecretError>`, `put(&self, key: &SecretKey, value: &SecretString) -> Result<(), SecretError>`, `delete(&self, key: &SecretKey) -> Result<(), SecretError>`, and `name(&self) -> &'static str`.

      - `copy_all(&self, from: ProfileId, to: ProfileId, retention: Retention) -> Result<(), SecretError>`

- **`hacivat-app::shell`** (Task 1 implements these fully; they are the extension points):
  - `Layer` gains `pub modal: bool`. `Layers::elements` scrims and occludes when `modal`, and `open_dialog` sets it. `Layers::has_modal(&self) -> bool` is working. The workspace passes `TitleBar::inert(self.layers.has_modal())`. Task 9 later moves the scrim below the title bar and makes `inert` take effect.
  - `ShellHooks` gains the following slots. Each has a `pub fn set_<name>(cx, …)`; with a slot empty, the M1 element renders.
    - `explorer: Option<ViewBuilder>`, `connection_panel: Option<ViewBuilder>`
    - `editor_empty: Option<ElementFn>`
    - `capsule: Option<CapsuleFn>`, `capsule_segment: Option<SegmentFn>`
    - `status_left: Option<StatusFn>`
    - `escape: Option<EscapeFn>`

    The types:
    - `ViewBuilder = Rc<dyn Fn(&mut Window, &mut App) -> AnyView>`
    - `ElementFn = Rc<dyn Fn(&Theme, &App) -> AnyElement>`
    - `CapsuleFn = Rc<dyn Fn(&App) -> Option<CapsuleModel>>`
    - `SegmentFn = Rc<dyn Fn(CapsuleSegment, Point<Pixels>, &mut Window, &mut App)>` (the point as in `Capsule::on_segment`)
    - `StatusFn = Rc<dyn Fn(&Theme, &App) -> Vec<AnyElement>>`
    - `EscapeFn = Rc<dyn Fn(&mut Window, &mut App) -> bool>`
  - `Workspace` builds the explorer and connection-panel views once in `new`, when those hooks are set. Hooks are therefore set before `open_workspace` (P17).
  - `pub fn register_focus_context(cx: &mut App, handle: FocusHandle, context: &'static str)` (in `shell`, not a `Workspace` method): it writes a `FocusContexts` Global, so a view built inside `Workspace::new` can register itself (P4).
  - `context_stack(&self, window: &Window, cx: &App) -> ContextStack` appends each registered context from `FocusContexts` whose `handle.is_focused(window)`. Its callers in `workspace.rs` and `tests/support/mod.rs` are updated.
  - `on_key_down`: on Escape with no layers open, call the `escape` hook, and stop propagation if it returns `true`.
  - While a modal layer is open, the dispatcher resolves only bindings of the top layer's context plus `app.quit` (P47).
  - `Workspace::open_anchored(&mut self, id: &'static str, anchor: Point<Pixels>, view: AnyView, window, cx)`: a non-modal layer with context `Popover`, dismissed by a press outside.
  - `Startup` gains `pub connections: Option<connections::ConnectionsBoot>`, `None` from `new`/`with_overrides`. `start` calls `connections::boot(cx, boot)` when `Some`, **before** `open_workspace`, so the hook views exist when `Workspace::new` builds them (P17).
- **`hacivat-app::connections`** (stub signatures; owners in brackets):
  - `pub fn install(cx: &mut App)`, an empty append-only registration block called from `crate::init`. It registers **actions only**, never hooks, so M1 tests and shots without a `ConnectionsBoot` render exactly as in M1 (P17).
  - `pub fn boot(cx: &mut App, boot: ConnectionsBoot)` (*working* wiring in T1): calls `boot::install(cx, boot)` [T12: sets the `ConnectionsModel` Global and shows the store-diagnostic toasts], then `live::install(cx)` [T18: the mirror task and every shell hook]. Both are no-op stubs in T1. Every hook view tolerates a missing `ConnectionsModel` by rendering the M1 element (P17).
  - `boot.rs` [T12]: `pub struct ConnectionsBoot { pub runtime: Arc<CoreRuntime>, pub store: ProfileStore, pub loaded: LoadedProfiles, pub secrets: Arc<SecretStore>, pub drivers: Arc<DriverRegistry>, pub private_dir: PathBuf }`, with `load(paths: &LocalPaths) -> ConnectionsBoot` (uses `MemoryBackend` when `HACIVAT_SECRETS=memory`) and `for_tests(paths: &LocalPaths, drivers: DriverRegistry) -> ConnectionsBoot` (memory backend).

- **Keymap data** (all platforms; `cmd-` on macOS, `ctrl-` elsewhere):

  | Context | Chord | Command |
  |---|---|---|
  | `Workspace` | `cmd-n` | `connection.new` |
  | `Workspace` | `cmd-shift-v` | `connection.paste_url` |
  | `Explorer` | `enter` | `connection.toggle_connect` |
  | `Explorer` | `cmd-e` | `connection.edit` |
  | `Explorer` | `cmd-d` | `connection.duplicate` |
  | `Explorer` | `cmd-shift-c` | `connection.copy_url` |

### Task 2: Engine catalog, driver registry, server info, sanitizing (HV-DRV-01, -05)

**Files:**
- Create: `crates/core/hacivat-driver/catalog/engines.toml`, `crates/core/hacivat-driver/tests/{catalog,sanitize}.rs`
- Modify: `crates/core/hacivat-driver/src/{catalog,registry,server,sanitize}.rs`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 1's `hacivat-driver` types and `hacivat-proto`.
- Runs in one agent with Task 3, T2 first (P14), with `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=<main checkout>/target-core/t02-t03`.
- Produces: the working `EngineCatalog::embedded()`, `EngineCatalog::engines()`, `DriverRegistry::resolve`, `SemVerLoose::parse`, `detect_mysql_family`, `support_status` (an engine with no floor is `Supported`, P4) and `sanitize`. The catalog content is normative for later tasks:
  - **`postgres`:** port 5432; floor `14`; tested `["14","15","16","17","18"]`; budget 4; auth `[password, pgpass, client_cert, none]`.
    - Conn types: `default`, `unix_socket`, `url_only`.
    - Defaults with rationale: `application_name=Hacivat`, `client_encoding=UTF8`, `extra_float_digits=3`.
    - Allow-list: `sslmode`, `application_name`, `connect_timeout`, `options`, `target_session_attrs`, `sslrootcert`, `sslcert`, `sslkey`.
    - Safe GUCs for `options`: `search_path`, `statement_timeout`, `application_name`, `DateStyle`, `TimeZone`, `lc_messages`.
  - **`mysql`:** port 3306; floor `8.0`; tested `["8.0","8.4"]`; budget 4; auth `[password, client_cert, none]`.
    - Always deny: `allowLoadLocalInfile`, `allowUrlInLocalInfile`, `autoDeserialize`.
    - Allow-list: `sslMode`, `useSSL`, `connectTimeout`, `characterEncoding`.
  - **`mariadb`:** fallback `mysql`; floor `10.6`; tested `["10.6","10.11","11.4"]`.
  - **`sqlite`:** no port; floor none; budget 1; auth `[none]`; conn types `file` and `memory`; always deny `vfs` and `load_extension`; allow-list `mode`.
  - Templates per conn type, using the HV-DRV-02 grammar.

```rust
#[test] fn embedded_catalog_has_the_v1_engines() {
    let c = EngineCatalog::embedded();
    assert_eq!(c.get(DbmsKind::Postgres).unwrap().default_port, Some(5432));
    assert_eq!(c.get(DbmsKind::MySql).unwrap().default_port, Some(3306));
    assert_eq!(c.get(DbmsKind::Sqlite).unwrap().default_port, None);
    assert_eq!(c.get(DbmsKind::Postgres).unwrap().default_budget, 4);

    assert_eq!(r.resolve(DbmsKind::MariaDb).unwrap().descriptor().kind, DbmsKind::MariaDb);

#[test] fn loose_versions_keep_raw() {
    for (raw, v) in [("16.15 (Debian 16.15-1.pgdg13+2)", (16,15,0)), ("8.0.46-0ubuntu", (8,0,46)),
                     ("10.11.6-MariaDB-1:10.11.6+maria~ubu2204", (10,11,6)), ("5.5.5-10.11.6-MariaDB", (10,11,6)), ("3.50.4", (3,50,4))] {
        let s = SemVerLoose::parse(raw).unwrap(); assert_eq!((s.major, s.minor, s.patch), v); assert_eq!(s.raw, raw);
    }
    assert_eq!(SemVerLoose::parse("devel"), None);
}
#[test] fn mysql_family_detection() {
    assert_eq!(detect_mysql_family("8.4.3", "MySQL Community Server - GPL"), DbmsKind::MySql);
    assert_eq!(detect_mysql_family("10.11.6-MariaDB", "mariadb.org binary distribution"), DbmsKind::MariaDb);
    assert_eq!(detect_mysql_family("11.4.2-MariaDB-ubu2404", ""), DbmsKind::MariaDb);
}
#[test] fn support_floors() {
    use Support::*; let c = EngineCatalog::embedded();
    for (k, v, want) in [(DbmsKind::Postgres, "13.18", Unsupported { floor: "14".into() }), (DbmsKind::Postgres, "14.0", Supported),
        (DbmsKind::Postgres, "18.1", Supported), (DbmsKind::Postgres, "19.0", NotTested), (DbmsKind::MySql, "5.7.44", Unsupported { floor: "8.0".into() }),
        (DbmsKind::MySql, "8.0.46", Supported), (DbmsKind::MySql, "8.4.3", Supported), (DbmsKind::MySql, "9.1.0", NotTested),
        (DbmsKind::MariaDb, "10.5.27", Unsupported { floor: "10.6".into() }), (DbmsKind::MariaDb, "11.4.4", Supported), (DbmsKind::MariaDb, "11.2.6", NotTested),
        (DbmsKind::Sqlite, "3.50.4", Supported)] {
        assert_eq!(support_status(c, &info(k, v)), want, "{k:?} {v}");
    }
    assert_eq!(support_status(c, &ServerInfo { version: None, ..info(DbmsKind::Postgres, "") }), NotTested);
}
```

- [ ] **Step 2:** Run `cargo test -p hacivat-driver --features fake --test catalog --test sanitize`. Expected: FAIL.
- [ ] **Step 3: Implement** `EngineCatalog::parse` (serde + toml; DFS with colour marking for cycles), `embedded()` (`include_str!` + `OnceLock`), `DriverRegistry::resolve`, `SemVerLoose::parse`, `detect_mysql_family`, `support_status` and `sanitize`.
  - `SemVerLoose::parse` strips a `5.5.5-` handshake prefix and reads the first `\d+(\.\d+){0,2}` run in ASCII digits.
  - `support_status` compares `major` (and `minor` for MySQL/MariaDB) against `floor` and `tested`. An engine with `floor = None` (SQLite) is `Supported`; a missing version on an engine with a floor is `NotTested`.
  - `sanitize` follows HV-DRV-05 steps 1–5: ASCII-lowercase keys; decode once and reject any `%XX` left; canonicalize paths, resolving symlinks; and apply the always-deny rules before the allow-list.

```rust
#[test] fn compile_vectors() {
    let t = compile("postgresql://{host:host:localhost}[:{port:port:5432}]", "default").unwrap();
    assert_eq!(t.field_set, [FieldId::Host, FieldId::Port].into());
    assert_eq!(t.default_of(FieldId::Host), Some("localhost")); assert_eq!(t.default_of(FieldId::Port), Some("5432"));
    assert_eq!(compile("{host:ipv6:\\:\\:1}", "ipv6").unwrap().default_of(FieldId::Host), Some("::1"));
    assert!(compile("<,{:ident}={:param}>", "x").unwrap().has_passthrough);
    assert_eq!(compile("{host", "x").unwrap_err(), TemplateError::Unbalanced { pos: 0 });
    assert_eq!(compile("{hots}", "x").unwrap_err(), TemplateError::UnknownField { name: "hots".into() });
}
#[test] fn render_display_and_copy() {
    let t = pg_uri();   // "postgresql://[{user}[:{password}]@]{host:host}[:{port:port}]/{database}[\?<&,{:param}={:param}>]"
    assert_eq!(render(&t, &f(&[("host","db"),("database","app")]), &[], RenderTarget::Display { has_password: false }).unwrap(), "postgresql://db/app");
    assert_eq!(render(&t, &f(&[("host","db"),("database","app")]), &[("sslmode".into(),"require".into())], RenderTarget::Copy).unwrap(), "postgresql://db/app?sslmode=require");
    let u = f(&[("host","db"),("database","app"),("user","a@b")]);
    assert_eq!(render(&t, &u, &[], RenderTarget::Display { has_password: true }).unwrap(), "postgresql://a%40b:****@db/app");
    assert_eq!(render(&t, &u, &[], RenderTarget::Copy).unwrap(), "postgresql://a%40b@db/app");
    assert_eq!(render(&t, &f(&[("host","::1"),("database","app")]), &[], RenderTarget::Copy).unwrap(), "postgresql://[::1]/app");
    assert_eq!(render(&t, &f(&[("host","db"),("database","данные")]), &[], RenderTarget::Copy).unwrap(), "postgresql://db/%D0%B4%D0%B0%D0%BD%D0%BD%D1%8B%D0%B5");
    assert_eq!(render(&t, &f(&[("database","app")]), &[], RenderTarget::Copy).unwrap_err(), RenderError::MissingField(FieldId::Host));
}
#[test] fn display_and_copy_never_contain_the_password() {
    let pw = SecretString::from("p:w@/%x"); let fm = f(&[("host","db"),("database","app"),("user","u")]);
    for target in [RenderTarget::Display { has_password: true }, RenderTarget::Copy] {
        let s = render(&pg_uri(), &fm, &[], target).unwrap(); assert!(!s.contains("p:w") && !s.contains("p%3Aw"), "{s}");
    }
    assert_eq!(render_driver(&pg_uri(), &fm, &[], &pw).unwrap().expose_secret(), "postgresql://u:p%3Aw%40%2F%25x@db/app");
}
#[test] fn rewrite_endpoint_vectors() {
    assert_eq!(rewrite_endpoint(&pg_uri(), "postgresql://db.example:5432/my%20db?host=db.example&port=5432#x", "127.0.0.1", 49505).unwrap(),
               "postgresql://127.0.0.1:49505/my%20db?host=db.example&port=5432#x");
    assert!(matches!(rewrite_endpoint(&pg_uri(), "postgresql://first:5432,second:5432/demo", "127.0.0.1", 49505), Err(RenderError::CannotRedirect(_))));
}
#[test] fn matcher_whole_input_scheme_case_and_budget() {
    let t = compile("jdbc:mysql://{host:host}[:{port:port}]/{database}", "jdbc").unwrap();
    assert_eq!(match_template(&t, "JDBC:mysql://h:3307/d").unwrap().0.values[&FieldId::Port], "3307");
    assert!(match_template(&t, "jdbc:mysql://h/d trailing junk").is_none());
    let long = "a".repeat(64 * 1024 + 1); let start = Instant::now();
    assert!(match_template(&t, &long).is_none()); assert!(start.elapsed() < Duration::from_millis(50));
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement** compile, render, the matcher and rewrite, following HV-DRV-02/03.
  - The parser is recursive descent with context stop sets.
  - Regexes use `regex::RegexBuilder::size_limit(1 << 20)`.
  - Percent-encoding escapes `@ / ? # % : [ ] ;` and space; UTF-8 is percent-encoded.
  - An optional group renders only when it has a user value, not just a default.
  - Only the scheme is matched ASCII-case-insensitively.

**Files:**

- Create: `crates/core/hacivat-secrets/tests/{store,scrub}.rs`, `scripts/dev-sign.sh`, `scripts/dev-run.sh`

**Interfaces:**
- Builds with `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=<main checkout>/target-core/t04` (P14).
- Consumes: Task 1's types, including `MemoryBackend::{new, with_max_blob}`.
- Produces: the working `SecretStore` (R8 retention semantics), `KeyringBackend` (including `available()` and `persistent()`), `scrub_url` and `scrub_options`. The store's retention rules:
  - `Forever`: backend only.
  - `UntilRestart`: a process map.
  - `ForSession`: a map cleared by `forget_session`.
  - `Never`: `put` returns `AsRequested` and keeps nothing.
  - When the backend is missing or fails with `Unavailable`, `Denied` or `TooLarge`, a `Forever` put becomes `FellBackTo { retention: UntilRestart, reason }`.

```rust
#[test] fn until_restart_never_touches_backend() { let (s, b) = store(); s.put(&k(), sec("x"), Retention::UntilRestart).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(b.calls(), 0); assert_eq!(s.get(&k(), Retention::UntilRestart).unwrap().unwrap().expose_secret(), "x"); }
#[test] fn for_session_is_forgotten() { let (s, _) = store(); s.put(&k(), sec("x"), Retention::ForSession).unwrap(); s.forget_session(k().profile);
    assert!(s.get(&k(), Retention::ForSession).unwrap().is_none()); }
#[test] fn never_keeps_nothing() { let (s, b) = store(); assert_eq!(s.put(&k(), sec("x"), Retention::Never).unwrap(), Stored::AsRequested);
    assert_eq!(b.calls(), 0); assert!(s.get(&k(), Retention::Never).unwrap().is_none()); }
#[test] fn lowering_retention_deletes_the_keyring_entry() { let (s, b) = store(); s.put(&k(), sec("x"), Retention::Forever).unwrap();
    s.change_retention(&k(), Retention::Forever, Retention::ForSession).unwrap(); assert!(b.get(&k()).unwrap().is_none());
    assert_eq!(s.get(&k(), Retention::ForSession).unwrap().unwrap().expose_secret(), "x"); }
#[test] fn delete_all_removes_every_slot() { let (s, b) = store(); let p = k().profile;
    let (k_db, k_tls) = (SecretKey { profile: p, slot: SecretSlot::DbPassword }, SecretKey { profile: p, slot: SecretSlot::TlsKeyPassphrase });
    s.put(&k_db, sec("a"), Retention::Forever).unwrap(); s.put(&k_tls, sec("b"), Retention::Forever).unwrap(); s.put(&k_db, sec("c"), Retention::ForSession).unwrap();
    s.delete_all(p).unwrap();
    assert!(b.get(&k_db).unwrap().is_none()); assert!(b.get(&k_tls).unwrap().is_none()); assert!(s.get(&k_db, Retention::ForSession).unwrap().is_none()); }
#[test] fn copy_all_duplicates_to_new_profile() { let (s, b) = store(); let (p1, p2) = (ProfileId::new(), ProfileId::new());
    s.put(&SecretKey { profile: p1, slot: SecretSlot::DbPassword }, sec("x"), Retention::Forever).unwrap();
    s.copy_all(p1, p2, Retention::Forever).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(b.get(&SecretKey { profile: p2, slot: SecretSlot::DbPassword }).unwrap().unwrap().expose_secret(), "x");
    assert_eq!(b.get(&SecretKey { profile: p1, slot: SecretSlot::DbPassword }).unwrap().unwrap().expose_secret(), "x"); }
#[test] fn keyring_denied_falls_back_to_session_with_reason() {
    let s = SecretStore::new(Some(Arc::new(Failing(SecretError::Denied))));
    assert_eq!(s.put(&k(), sec("x"), Retention::Forever).unwrap(), Stored::FellBackTo { retention: Retention::UntilRestart, reason: SecretError::Denied });
    assert_eq!(s.get(&k(), Retention::Forever).unwrap().unwrap().expose_secret(), "x");
}
#[test] fn windows_blob_limit_falls_back() {
    let s = SecretStore::new(Some(Arc::new(MemoryBackend::with_max_blob(2560))));
    assert!(matches!(s.put(&k(), sec(&"a".repeat(2561)), Retention::Forever).unwrap(), Stored::FellBackTo { reason: SecretError::TooLarge { limit: 2560 }, .. }));
}
#[test] fn service_names() { assert_eq!(key("6f9619ff-8b86-d011-b42d-00c04fc964ff", SecretSlot::DbPassword).service(),
    if cfg!(debug_assertions) { "hacivat-dev/6f9619ff-8b86-d011-b42d-00c04fc964ff/db-password" } else { "hacivat/6f9619ff-8b86-d011-b42d-00c04fc964ff/db-password" }); }
#[test] fn secrets_never_debug_print() {   // our types, not secrecy itself (P33); ProfileDraft, ParseOutcome and FakeRequest are covered by Task 1's debug_redaction test

    assert!(KeyringBackend::persistent(), "keyring's default store is the non-persistent mock: check the apple-native / windows-native / async-secret-service features");

#[test] #[ignore = "keyring-live"] fn live_keyring_round_trip() {  // HACIVAT_KEYRING_LIVE=1; dev-signed binary on macOS
    let b = KeyringBackend::new(None); let key = SecretKey { profile: ProfileId::new(), slot: SecretSlot::DbPassword };
    b.put(&key, &sec("hv-live")).unwrap(); assert_eq!(b.get(&key).unwrap().unwrap().expose_secret(), "hv-live");

#[test] fn scrub_vectors() {
    assert_eq!(scrub_url("postgres://u:p@h/db"), "postgres://u@h/db");
    assert_eq!(scrub_url("mysql://root:pw@h:3306/db"), "mysql://root@h:3306/db");
    assert_eq!(scrub_url("jdbc:postgresql://h/db?user=u&password=p"), "jdbc:postgresql://h/db?user=u");
    assert_eq!(scrub_url("host=h password='a b' dbname=d"), "host=h dbname=d");
    assert_eq!(scrub_url("Server=h;Password=x;"), "Server=h;");
    assert_eq!(scrub_options(&[("sslpassword".into(),"x".into()), ("sslmode".into(),"require".into())]),
               vec![("sslpassword".into(), "<removed>".into()), ("sslmode".into(), "require".into())]);
}
```

- [ ] **Step 2:** Run `cargo test -p hacivat-secrets --test store --test scrub`. Expected: FAIL.
- [ ] **Step 3: Implement** the store, the backends and the scrub functions.
  - `KeyringBackend`: `keyring::Entry::new(&key.service(), "hacivat")`. Map `NoEntry` to `Ok(None)`. Map `PlatformFailure` and `NoStorageAccess` to `Denied` or `Unavailable`, and `TooLong` to `TooLarge`. On Windows, `max_blob = Some(2560)`. Where the platform credential supports a comment or label, set it to the profile name (HV-SEC-05, P44); on macOS keyring 3 ignores it, which is acceptable. Never call `keyring::set_default_credential_builder`.
  - `KeyringBackend::available()` (P36): true on macOS and Windows; on Linux, probe the D-Bus session for the name `org.freedesktop.secrets` without prompting. `SecretStore::keyring_available()` computes it once, off the UI thread, and caches it.
  - `KeyringBackend::persistent()`: `keyring::default::default_credential_builder().persistence() == CredentialPersistence::UntilDelete`. The mock reports `EntryOnly`.
  - `scrub_options` removes the values of keys matching `(?i)pass|pwd|secret|token|key`, using ASCII-insensitive matching.
  - The dev scripts follow R7.
    - `dev-sign.sh --create-identity`: uses `security` and `openssl` to make a self-signed code-signing certificate named "Hacivat Dev" in the login keychain, then runs `security add-trusted-cert -p codeSign` on it (one admin prompt; documented in the script's header). It is idempotent.
    - `dev-run.sh <bin> [args]`: runs `codesign --force --sign "Hacivat Dev" --identifier dev.hacivat.app <bin>` when the identity exists, then `exec`s the binary. If the identity is missing or `codesign` fails for any reason, it prints one hint on stderr and `exec`s the binary unsigned (P35). It wraps every macOS `cargo run`, `cargo test` and `cargo xtask`, including CI's, so it must never fail.
    - `.cargo/config.toml` keeps the `xtask` alias and gains `[target.aarch64-apple-darwin] runner` and `[target.x86_64-apple-darwin] runner` set to `scripts/dev-run.sh`.

### Task 5: Byte streams, direct transport, loopback forwarder, non-interactive TLS (HV-CONN-01 transport, HV-SEC-02 modes)

**Files:**
- Modify: `crates/core/hacivat-net/src/{stream,direct,address,forwarder,tls}.rs`
- Create: `crates/core/hacivat-net/tests/{address,direct,forwarder,tls}.rs`

**Interfaces:**
- Builds with `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=<main checkout>/target-core/t05` (P14).
- Consumes: Task 1's types, and the working `install_crypto_provider` (every test that builds a TLS config calls it first).
- Produces:
  - `resolve` (`tokio::net::lookup_host`; a failure is `NetError::Dns`). An IP literal resolves to itself without a DNS query.
  - `direct`: Happy Eyeballs with a 250 ms stagger (RFC 8305), alternating families, and an overall `connect_timeout`. It returns a `StreamFactory` that opens a new socket on every call.
  - `classify_ip` and `classify_host`. Private means RFC 1918, RFC 4193 and link-local. CGNAT `100.64/10` is Public. A host is Public if any resolved address is public.
  - `LoopbackForwarder` (R18), in both `ForwarderMode`s: `Tcp` always binds `127.0.0.1:0` (the MySQL path, P10); `UnixOrTcp` binds a `0600` Unix socket in a `0700` directory on Unix and `127.0.0.1:0` on Windows. It accepts at most `max_accepts` connections; the first `factory.open()` result goes to the first accepted connection.
  - `build_tls_params`. rustls uses the ring provider and `rustls-platform-verifier`, plus the CA file roots, with protocol versions `[TLS13, TLS12]`. The verifier depends on the mode:
    - `VerifyFull`: the platform verifier, or `WebPkiServerVerifier` over the platform roots plus the CA file.
    - `VerifyCa`: chain-only, with a custom verifier that skips the hostname.
    - `Require`: accepts any certificate.

    Every verifier writes its first error, mapped to `TlsFailure`, into `TlsParams::failure`. `server_name` is `ServerName::IpAddress` for IP literals (no SNI). `plaintext_allowed = (mode == Auto) && class != Public`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests:**
```rust
#[test] fn address_classes() {
    for (ip, c) in [("127.0.0.1", Loopback), ("::1", Loopback), ("10.1.2.3", Private), ("172.16.0.1", Private), ("172.32.0.1", Public),
                    ("192.168.1.1", Private), ("fd00::1", Private), ("169.254.1.1", Private), ("100.64.0.1", Public), ("8.8.8.8", Public)] {
        assert_eq!(classify_ip(ip.parse().unwrap()), c, "{ip}");
    }
    assert_eq!(classify_host("localhost", &[]), Loopback);
    assert_eq!(classify_host("db", &[sa("10.0.0.5:5432"), sa("8.8.8.8:5432")]), Public);
}
#[tokio::test] async fn direct_connects_and_falls_back_to_the_next_address() {
    let (open, closed) = (echo_server().await, closed_port());
    let f = direct(Target::Tcp { host: "localhost".into(), port: open.port() }, vec![closed, open], Duration::from_secs(2));
    let mut s = f.open().await.unwrap(); s.write_all(b"ping").await.unwrap(); assert_eq!(read4(&mut s).await, *b"ping");
}
#[tokio::test] async fn refused_and_dns_errors() {
    assert!(matches!(resolve("nonexistent.invalid", 5432).await, Err(NetError::Dns { .. })));
    let f = direct(Target::Tcp { host: "localhost".into(), port: 1 }, vec![closed_port()], Duration::from_secs(1));
    assert_eq!(f.open().await.unwrap_err().kind(), io::ErrorKind::ConnectionRefused);
}
#[tokio::test] async fn forwarder_relays_counts_and_cleans_up() {
    let dir = tempdir(); let (fwd, guard) = LoopbackForwarder::bind(echo_factory().await, dir.path(), 4, ForwarderMode::UnixOrTcp).await.unwrap();
    let mut c = connect_to(fwd.addr()).await; c.write_all(b"ping").await.unwrap(); assert_eq!(read4(&mut c).await, *b"ping");
    assert_eq!(fwd.accepted(), 1);
    #[cfg(unix)] if let ForwarderAddr::Unix(p) = fwd.addr() { assert_eq!(mode(p) & 0o777, 0o600); assert_eq!(mode(p.parent().unwrap()) & 0o777, 0o700); }
    guard.run().await; assert!(connect_to_result(fwd.addr()).await.is_err());
}
#[tokio::test] async fn tcp_mode_forwarder_is_loopback_tcp_and_accepts_once() {
    let dir = tempdir(); let (fwd, _guard) = LoopbackForwarder::bind(echo_factory().await, dir.path(), 1, ForwarderMode::Tcp).await.unwrap();
    let ForwarderAddr::Tcp(a) = fwd.addr() else { panic!("expected TCP, got {:?}", fwd.addr()) }; assert!(a.ip().is_loopback());
    let mut c = connect_to(fwd.addr()).await; c.write_all(b"ping").await.unwrap(); assert_eq!(read4(&mut c).await, *b"ping");
    assert!(connect_to_result(fwd.addr()).await.is_err()); assert_eq!(fwd.accepted(), 1);
}
#[tokio::test] async fn tls_modes_against_a_self_signed_server() {
    let srv = rcgen_server("localhost").await;                       // self-signed leaf
    let err = handshake(&srv, TlsMode::VerifyFull, None, "localhost").await.unwrap_err();
    assert_eq!(err.failure, Some(TlsFailure::UnknownIssuer));
    assert!(handshake(&srv, TlsMode::Require, None, "localhost").await.is_ok());
    let ca_srv = rcgen_ca_server("other.example").await;              // CA-signed leaf for another name
    assert!(handshake(&ca_srv, TlsMode::VerifyCa, Some(&ca_srv.ca_pem), "localhost").await.is_ok());
    assert_eq!(handshake(&ca_srv, TlsMode::VerifyFull, Some(&ca_srv.ca_pem), "localhost").await.unwrap_err().failure, Some(TlsFailure::NameMismatch));
}
#[test] fn tls_params_shape() {
    assert!(build_tls_params(&settings(TlsMode::Disable), "db", Public).unwrap().is_none());
    assert!(build_tls_params(&settings(TlsMode::Auto), "localhost", Loopback).unwrap().unwrap().plaintext_allowed);
    assert!(!build_tls_params(&settings(TlsMode::Auto), "db.acme.io", Public).unwrap().unwrap().plaintext_allowed);
    let p = build_tls_params(&settings(TlsMode::VerifyFull), "127.0.0.1", Loopback).unwrap().unwrap();
    assert!(matches!(p.server_name, ServerName::IpAddress(_)));
    assert_eq!(build_tls_params(&with_ca(TlsMode::VerifyCa, "/nope.pem"), "db", Public).unwrap_err(), TlsSetupError::CaFileUnreadable("/nope.pem".into()));
    assert_eq!(build_tls_params(&with_client_key(ENCRYPTED_PKCS8_PEM), "db", Public).unwrap_err(), TlsSetupError::KeyEncrypted(key_path()));
}
```

### Task 6: UI form primitives (NOTES.md "NEW primitives": dialog sizes, checkbox, select, disclosure, inline notice, choice list)

**Files:**
- Modify: `crates/ui/hacivat-ui/src/{checkbox,select,disclosure,notice,choice_list,form,dialog,segmented,menu}.rs`, `crates/ui/hacivat-ui/src/input/mod.rs`
- Create: `crates/ui/hacivat-ui/tests/form_primitives.rs`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 1's signatures, M1's `Button`, `PopupMenu` anatomy, `popover_surface`, `focus_outline`, `Label` and `TextFn`.
- Produces: working primitives whose metrics and tokens match the NOTES.md "NEW primitives" table. **That table is normative; copy its numbers.**
  - The **dialog form** variant: a header with padding 12/16 and a seam below; a scrolling body with padding 12/16/14; a footer with padding 12/16 and a seam above.
  - **Select** embeds an M1 `PopupMenu` entity inside its own `deferred(anchored())`, not as a workspace layer, so the row anatomy (24 px rows, 200 px minimum, hover, check column) is not duplicated (P34). `deferred` paints with no content mask at the pin, so a scrolling dialog body does not clip it. `MenuItemSpec` gains `checked: bool` and `hint: Option<TextFn>` (set by its constructors). Select subscribes to `PopupMenuEvent::Chosen`, with each item's action set to its index. While open it holds focus and stops propagation of Escape, ↑, ↓ and Enter, so the dialog layer under it stays open.
  - **Segmented** gains roving arrow keys (←/→ move and press). There is still one tab stop per group.
  - **ChoiceList** uses ↑/↓ to move and select.
  - **`TextInput::set_masked(true)`** paints `•` per grapheme. Copy and cut do nothing. Its accessible value is empty. `value()` is unchanged.
  - **`TextInput::set_paste_filter`** (P16): on paste, a non-masked input calls the filter with the clipboard text; `true` consumes the paste (nothing is inserted), `false` inserts it as before. A masked input never calls the filter, so the Password rule of R21 holds in the widget. Escape keeps M1's behaviour (`InputEvent::Cancelled`, propagation stopped).
